Pseudo:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def safe_evaluate(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf

def clamp_sequence(seq):
    out=[]
    for x in seq:
       try:
          y=float(x)
       except Exception:
          continue
       if not np.isfinite(y): continue
       if y<0: y=0.0
       if y>1000: y=1000.0
       out.append(y)
    return out
```
No lambda/closure.

def maybe_improve_local(best, budget_s, deadline):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_arr = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64)
    n = best_arr.size
    if n == 0 or n > 50000: return best
    # if best_score not finite return
    # Use small block multiplicative perturbations; only accept if lower.
    attempts = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1:
       attempts +=1
       if attempts > 2000: break
       cand = best_arr.copy()
       width = max(2, min(n//16, 64))
       start = rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1))
       factor = rng.lognormal(0.0, 0.05)
       cand[start:start+width] *= factor
       # optionally symmetrize
       cand = 0.5*(cand + cand[::-1])
       cand = np.clip(cand, 0, 1000)
       score = safe_evaluate(cand.tolist())
       if finite and score < best_score:
          best_arr = cand; best_score=score
       if best_score <= 2.0 - 1e-14: break? Maybe.
```

We need create an effective optimizer. Let's formalize.

Given n, nonnegative vector a length n. Objective f(a)= (2n/S^2) max_k (a*a)_k. Scale invariant; set S=1. Then f=2n max c_k. Need minimize max c_k. Since total sum c_k =1. The state-of-art approach: Starting f normalized sum sqrt(2n), M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP maximizing sum g (nonnegative) subject to ||f*g||∞ ≤ M. Rescale g to same sum, update f = (1-t)f+t g. This is like a Frank-Wolfe / multiplicative weights / mirror descent? It uses the current convolution as constraints and finds a new sequence that has large total mass while not exceeding current peak when convolved with current sequence. Iterating lowers peak. The LP method more reliable. Let's see if we can implement an efficient approximation to the LP update without full LP: The optimal g for fixed f maximizes sum g subject to f*g≤P. This is a linear program with a nonnegative matrix with a special structure (shifts of f). Since f is nonnegative, the constraint matrix columns are shifted f. The LP resembles finding a nonnegative sequence g of maximum l1 such that convolution with f is bounded by P. This is equivalent to a "capacity" problem on a path. There may be a greedy solution! Let's investigate.

Given f≥0 length n, P. We need max ∑g_i subject to for each k, ∑_{i=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(k,n-1)} f_{k-i} g_i ≤ P, g_i≥0.
This is a linear program representing flow in a network? Constraints are sliding windows with weights f. Max total weight g. The LP dual: min P∑ y_k subject to for each i, ∑_{k=i}^{i+n-1} f_{k-i} y_k ≥1, y_k≥0. This is like placing weights y on sequence positions to cover each shift i with weighted sum ≥1, minimizing total (times P). If f were uniform, primal max sum g = (2n-1)P? Because constraints each window sum g ≤P; with f=1/(sqrt?)? For uniform f_i=1/n, constraint (f*g)_k = (1/n)∑ window g ≤P => window sum ≤nP. Max ∑g? For n=2, f=[0.5,0.5], P=current max? Primal max sum g maybe? Not greedy.

This LP is a covering/packing that may be solved by dynamic programming if f has special shape? General f 1D, constraints are interval knapsack. Could use simplex? scipy OK.

But we may not need exact LP; a heuristic g can improve. What if we set g_i proportional to 1 / max over k of f_{k-i}? Because to satisfy f*g≤P, large g_i can be limited by large f components. Maximize sum with constraints; if constraints independent, g_i≈P/max_j f_j. More precisely, a feasible g is g_i = η / m_i where m_i = max_{k valid} f_{k-i}. Then (f*g)_k = ∑ f_{k-i} η/m_i ≤ η ∑ f_{k-i}/m_i. The worst is ≤? If choose η = P / max_k ∑ f_{k-i}/m_i, then feasible and sum=η∑1/m_i. This is a heuristic. P can be set to 1? The scaled g sum maybe. For f with support, m_i is max f value (same for all i) if f window shifted? Actually m_i = max_{j=0..n-1} f_j (for all i at least one window includes all? Window length varies; endpoints may not include all f. But for interior i, yes. Endpoints maybe less. If f max near endpoint, endpoints m smaller. This heuristic might find g concentrated where f small.

Another heuristic: Use dual weights y active on max c. Then set g_i = exp(-γ ∑_k y_k f_{k-i}) (multiplicative weights). This is easy with FFT. Then scale g to sum 1, update f. This is a known multiplicative weights for the dual. Let's try to design:
- f normalized sum=1.
- c = f*f; P=max c.
- Choose active y_k = (c_k/P)^β or softmax over c_k (nonnegative), maybe normalized sum. This dual aims to penalize lags where current convolution high.
- Compute z_i = ∑_k y_k f_{k-i} = (y * f)_i? This is a convolution/correlation using same direction. Using FFT, z nonnegative if y,f≥0.
- Set g_i = exp(-η z_i) (or 1/(1+η z_i)). Since high z means increasing g_i would strongly hit active high lags, so reduce it.
- Normalize g sum=1.
- Update f = (1-t)f + t g.
This is a multiplicative weights/subgradient method. It may reduce peak. We can tune η, t, β. This is much faster (FFT O(n log n)). We can run many iterations for n=6962 within 2400s.

Let's test mentally with n=2. f=[1/3,2/3], c=[1/9,4/9,4/9], P=4/9. Choose y proportional to c maybe [0.1,0.45,0.45]/1? Normalize sum maybe 1. Compute z_i=∑ y_k f_{k-i}. For i=0: k=0 y0*f0 + k=1 y1*f1 + k=2 y2*f2? f2 invalid; =y0*0.333+y1*0.667 ≈0.033+0.300=0.333. i=1: k=1 y1*f0 + k=2 y2*f1 =0.15+0.30=0.45. g∝ exp(-η z): smaller at i=1 (where f large), so g shifts mass to endpoint 0. Updating f towards g might make distribution more skewed [2/3,1/3] maybe better? For n=2 optimum is [1/3,2/3] (or reverse) already. If f starts uniform [0.5,0.5], c=[0.25,0.5,0.25], P=0.5, y[0]=0.25, y1=0.5,y2=0.25. z0=0.25*0.5+0.5*0.5=0.375; z1=0.5*0.5+0.25*0.5=0.375; g uniform, no update. Need asymmetry to improve? Uniform is local? For n=2 optimal [1,2] but our method with symmetric y gives symmetric z; it can't break symmetry. Need random perturbation or active set not symmetric. If f uniform, c symmetric, any symmetric method stays uniform. Break symmetry by random small perturbations or by choosing y with directional bias. For large n, need asymmetry. The objective is not symmetric? Convolution c_k of a with itself is not symmetric under reversal? If a reversed, c reversed. Reversal gives same score. So there are symmetric pairs. Uniform symmetric is saddle; need random initialization.

- We want move a to reduce ∑ y c ≈P. Natural gradient on simplex: new a = a * exp(-η g) / sum.

Implementation details for FFT convolution and gradient:
```
def fft_convolution-square(a, L=None):
    n = len(a)
    L = 1
    need = 2*n -1
    while L < need: L <<=1
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:need]
    # remove tiny negative
    if c.min() < 0: c = np.clip(c, 0, None)
    return c
```
For gradient z_i = ∑_{k=0}^{need-1} y_k a_{k-i}. We can compute using FFT with reversed y:
```
def shift_weighted_gradient(a, y, L):
    # y length need=2n-1
    y_rev = y[::-1]
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    Y = np.fft.rfft(y_rev, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(F*Y, L)
    idx = (2*n -2) - np.arange(n)  # L-1 - i
    z = conv[idx]
    return np.clip(z,0,None)
```
Check index: need-1=2n-2. For i=0, index=2n-2. For i=n-1, index=n-1. conv array length L; should have linear conv of y_rev (length 2n-1) and a (n) length 3n-2. Index ranges 0..3n-3. For i=0, t=2n-2; term y_j a_{j}? Need verify with small n=2, a=[a0,a1], need=3, y=[y0,y1,y2], y_rev=[y2,y1,y0]. conv[t]=∑_{m} y_rev[m] a[t-m]. t=2: m=0 y2 a2 invalid (a2=0), m=1 y1 a1, m=2 y0 a0 => z0 = y0 a0 + y1 a1. Formula z0=∑_k y_k a_{k}: k=0 a0, k=1 a1, k=2 a2 invalid => y0a0+y1a1. good. i=1: t=1: m=0 y2 a1, m=1 y1 a0 => y1a0+y2a1. Formula z1= y1 a0? k=1 a0, k=2 a1 yes. Good.

Step update:
```
def log_normalize(theta):
    theta = theta - max(theta)
    val = np.exp(theta)
    s = val.sum()
    if s ==0 or not finite: return uniform
    return val/s
```

Let's design `optimize_sequence(a, budget seconds, seed, ...)`:

Pseudo:
```
def fft_norm(a):
    s = a.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.ones(n)/n
    return a/s

def conv_sq_fft(a):
    n=a.size; need=2*n-1; L=next_power_of_two(need)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:need]
    c[c<0]=0
    return c
```
Need `next_power_of_two` helper.

Optimization parameters:
- n = a.size
- `a` normalized.
- `L = next_power_of_two(2*n-1)`
- `active_tol`: start 0.1, maybe anneal to 0.01? Active weights based on `deltaP = max(active_tol*P, 10*machine_eps)`.
- `beta_effective = 1 / deltaP` if using exponential active.
- `step_base`: maybe 0.1. For natural gradient, too small slow; too large oscillate. We can adapt via line search.

Line search approach per iteration:
```
c = conv_sq_fft(a); P = c.max(); score_approx = 2*n*P
# compute active weights
exp_arg = (c - P + deltaP) / deltaP? If c>=P-deltaP, weight=exp((c-P)/(deltaP)), else 0.
w = ... / sum
z = gradient(a,w)
zmax = max(z)
# Try steps [0.5,1,2,4]*base maybe scaled by 1/zmax.
for mult in steps:
    eta = base * mult / zmax
    theta = np.log(a+1e-300) - eta*z
    a_new = normalize_exp(theta)
    a_new = (1-mix)*a_new + mix*uniform
    c_new = conv_sq_fft(a_new); P_new = c_new.max()
    if P_new < P: accept step_mult; break
If reject all, a = perturb random maybe? or reduce base.
```
This line search requires extra convex evals; with FFT cheap. But if base step appropriate, one or two trials. We can simplify: choose eta = step / zmax; if new score worse, reduce step; if better, increase.

Let's think about using `scipy.optimize.linprog` for the LP update in the actual solver. We can implement it as an optional polishing step for a given a of length n ≤ maybe 2048 (or 4096). The LP direction might be more effective. We can run a few iterations on the best sequence before final.

LP update function:
```
def lp_step(a, P, tol):
    n = len(a)
    # Build sparse A shape (2n-1, n), where A[k,i] = a_{k-i} if 0<=k-i<n
    rows=[]; cols=[]; data=[]
    for i in range(n):
       # k from i to i+n-1, value a[i+n-1? wait A[k,i]=a[k-i], j=k-i from 0..n-1
       # k = i+j, j=0..n-1
       rows.append(range(i, i+n))
       cols.append(range(n))
       data.append(a) # all lengths n; but for each i, column i has a[j] at row i+j.
```
We can build using scipy.sparse.bmat? Simpler vectorized:
For each i, rows = i + np.arange(n); col = np.zeros(n); but loops n=2000 okay (4M ops). For n=4096, 16M, okay memory building maybe.
A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2n-1,n))
Then linprog objective c = -ones(n) (minimize -sum), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2n-1,P), bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'presolve':'highs'?}

Algorithm `optimize(a, time_limit, seed)`:
1. Ensure a positive, normalize.
2. Precompute L, uniform vector.
3. `best_a = a.copy(); best_P = conv_sq_fft(best_a).max()`; best_score_exact maybe later.
4. `eta = 0.2`; `stall = 0`; `last_improve = time`
5. Main loop until time budget exceeded or no improvement for many (but continue random perturb).
   - Compute `c = conv_sq_fft(a)`; `P = c.max()`.
   - Choose active weights:
       `delta = max(0.02 * P, 1e-12)`.
       `arg = (c - (P - delta)) / delta` for c >= P-delta else -inf.
       `w = exp(arg)`; but if delta small and c plateau, many weights ~1. Sum maybe n; normalize w /= sum.
       If sum not finite, use top K.
   - Compute `z = gradient(a, w)`.
   - Scale `z` by `zmax = max(z)`; if zmax small, skip.
   - Try step multiplier list `[1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25, 4.0]` maybe. For each:
        `a_new = exp(normalize_log(a, -eta*mult*z/zmax))`
        `P_new = conv_sq_fft(a_new).max()`
        if P_new < P - 1e-15: accept, update eta *= 1.05 (cap), stall=0, break.
   - If no accept:
        `eta = max(0.01, eta*0.8)`; stall++
        If stall > some, apply random perturbation: `a *= exp(rng.normal(0, 0.02*n?)); normalize`; maybe if improves accept; else keep.
   - Update a=best? We can only accept improvements to maintain monotonic. But line search may fail due local flat; random perturb can explore.
6. To avoid monotonic getting stuck, we can allow simulated annealing: accept worse with probability exp(- (P_new-P)/temperature), but monitoring. ```
def random_move(a, rng, scale):
   # choose block or global noise
   if rng.random()<0.5:
      width = max(8, n//32)
      start = rng.integers(0,n-width)
      factor = rng.lognormal(0, scale)
      c = a.copy(); c[start:start+width]*=factor
   else:
      c = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0, scale, n))
   # optionally symmetrize? The optimal is asymmetric; don't sym.
   normalize, return
```
Accept if P_new < P (or within annealing). Use scale ~0.02 initially, decrease. This is similar previous but from improved start.

Let's derive a direct iterative algorithm from LP update without full LP: Use dual variables y from softmax of current max, set g_i = exp(-t y*f shifted). Update f. This is exactly our natural gradient with weights y and step (but without scaling/line search). The state-of-art LP would choose optimal g, which depends on all constraints, not just active y. We can improve by using a *best response* to y: In the LP dual, if we have y, the maximizing g for Lagrangian `sum g - λ (f*g - P)` is unbounded unless y≥0 and A^T y ≥ λ; complementary. But multiplicative weights on dual can solve. We can iterate dual y with multiplicative weights and primal g as follows:
- Initialize y uniform over lags.
- Primal g_i = 1 / (∑_k y_k A_{k,i})? Let's derive. For the LP max sum x s.t. A x ≤ 1, x≥0. A heuristic: Let y be nonnegative dual weights, and set x_i = 1 / max(1, A_i^T y). Then A x_k = ∑ A_{k,i}/max(1,A_i^T y). We can scale y to make this ≤P. But to maximize sum, maybe x_i ∝ 1/(A_i^T y)^p. This is similar to our exp(-η z). Increasing η approximates p=1? We can tune.

Maybe we can choose η large to make g concentrate where z small (where f*g constraints loose). Our update `g ∝ a exp(-η z)` includes current a as prior; LP g not constrained by a, so maybe we should optimize g independent of a: `g_i ∝ exp(-η z_i)` (or 1/(1+η z_i)) without multiplying by a. Then update f = mix(a, scale(g)). The state-of-art LP maximizes sum g; starting from uniform? The optimal g may not be close to a. If we use prior a, we move slowly. Could use g = exp(-η z) normalized, then update. This might be more effective. Let's test: For fixed f, z_i = ∑ y_k f_{k-i}. If y active max lags, z indicates how much mass at i would contribute to high lags. We want g large where z small. The natural gradient prior a might not allow redistributing mass far if a_i tiny? Multiplying by a_i means if a_i is tiny at some coordinate, even if z small, g_i remains tiny; mass cannot move into underused coordinates. The LP g is unconstrained (except nonnegative) and can allocate to any coordinate. Thus we should not multiply by a; instead set a new distribution proportional to exp(-η z_i) (or a soft max). Then update f as convex combination. This is more like the LP heuristics.

But to reduce objective, if g has low scores when convolved with f (f*g≤P?), maybe new f = mix improves. Let's formulate:
- Given a (sum1), P=max a*a. Choose y over active lags.
- Define g_i ∝ exp(-η z_i) (sum1). This distribution puts mass on coordinates that don't increase active lags much.
- Update a ← (1-t)a + t g.
This is a Markov chain / mirror descent. It can move mass arbitrarily. We need choose η and t. This might be more powerful than multiplicative updates. But LP g can allocate mass to regions a small; exp(-η z) can. Let's use a hybrid: `g = (1-μ) * a + μ * exp_normalize(-eta*z)`? Or simply `g = exp_normalize(-eta*z)`, then update a=(1-t)a+t g. This is a discrete-time Markov algorithm. Step t controls movement. The exp distribution may be very skewed if η large.

Which is better? The natural gradient `a_new = a exp(-step z)` is like infinitesimal move from a. The `g` method can make larger moves. We can use both: for small t, similar with log(a - t z)? Because log((1-t)a + t g) not.

Maybe implement a "convex mixture" update:
```
g = exp_normalize(-eta * z / zscale)
a_candidate = (1 - alpha) * a + alpha * g
```
where alpha step (0.1-0.5). This maintains nonnegative and can redistribute mass significantly. It's simple and likely effective. We can line search alpha. This resembles a Frank-Wolfe step where g is a direction from a simple response rather than exact LP.

The state-of-art uses g from LP (max sum under constraints), not just exp(-z). The exp(-z) is a multiplicative weights approximation to the LP solution if η large? For fixed y, the LP primal with constraints A x ≤ P and max sum has solution where if y is dual, complementary slackness: x_i>0 only if A_i^T y = something. The distribution x_i ∝ exp(-η A_i^T y) as η→∞ puts min A_i^T y; not the same as maximizing sum. But maybe okay.

We can improve by using `g_i = 1/(1 + η z_i)` instead of exp. As η large, g supports min z. The sum of g maybe. Normalizing to sum1. This is a soft minimization of z. It may be more stable.

Let's detail top-level helpers:
- `next_power_of_two(m)`: while.
- `positive_normalize(v)`: clip nonnegative, sum, if sum<=0 return uniform.
- `central_binomial_length(n)`: recurrence to array float64 length n.
   a[0]=1; for i=1..n-1: a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i).
- `make_initial_sequence(n, seed, alpha=0.0)`: cb with optional tilt; normalize.
- `convolution_square_fft(a, L=None)`: returns real c length 2n-1 and L maybe.
- `gradient_from_weights(a, w, L)`: returns z length n.
- `active_weights(c, P, tol=0.05, temp=0.02)`: returns w length need sum1.
- `exp_normalize(theta)`: stable.
- `log_normalize_from_log(log_a, ...)`.
- `score_approx(a, L)`: compute c and return 2n*maxc maybe.
- `exact_score_list(seq)`: safe_evaluate.
- `random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)`.
- `lp_stochastic_step(a, c, P, rng, n, K, max_calls?, time_limit?)`: but this function may need its own time. We can integrate into main loop.

Now, simulated annealing/random perturb. We can implement a simple "move plus line search":
`random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)`:
- Choose block width between n//100 and n//8, factor lognormal(0,sigma) applied to block; sometimes global noise sigma small.
- Normalize, compute P. Accept if P < current_P - tol or with annealing prob.
We will use it when no improvement for e.g. 10 iterations. Annealing temperature: `temp = max(1e-6*P, 1e-12)`. Accept worse if `P_new - P < temp and rng.random() < exp(-diff/temp)`. But keep best separate. To avoid wasting, limit random moves.

Maybe not needed if LP provides exploration.

Let's outline `optimize( initial, time_limit, seed, exact_best_tracker?)`:
```
def optimize_sequence(initial, time_budget, seed=0, label='', do_exact=True, exact_budget_limit=...):
    a = positive_normalize(np.asarray(initial,float))
    n = a.size
    if n < 2: return a, safe_evaluate(a.tolist())
    deadline = time.monotonic() + time_budget
    L = next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    c = conv_sq_fft(a,L); P = c.max(); best_a=a.copy(); best_P=P
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    eta = 3.0; alpha = 0.3
    failures=0; no_improve_time=time.monotonic(); last_lp_time=0; lp_count=0
    iterations=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       c = conv_sq_fft(a,L)  # maybe update if not
       P = c.max()
       if P < best_P - 1e-15:
           best_P=P; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve_time=now
       # active weights
       w = active_weights(c,P)
       z = gradient(a,w,L)
       zmax = z.max()
       if zmax > 0 and finite:
           zn = z / zmax
           g = exp_normalize(-eta * zn)
           cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*g
           cand = positive_normalize(cand)
           P_cand = conv_sq_fft(cand,L).max()
           if P_cand < P - 1e-14:
               a = cand; P = P_cand
               if P < best_P: best...
               failures=0
               eta = min(20.0, eta*1.03)
               alpha = min(0.9, alpha*1.02)
           else:
               failures +=1
               eta = max(1.0, eta*0.9)
               alpha = max(0.05, alpha*0.95)
               # if failures high, try alternative multiplicative candidate
               if failures % 10 == 0:
                  step = max(0.05, eta*0.1)  # ?
                  cand2 = exp_normalize(np.log(a+1e-300) - (eta*0.2?)*zn)
                  ... accept if improves
       else: break
       failures +=? (if no candidate)
       # If no improvement for 100 iterations or 30s, try LP
       if P >= best_P - 1e-15 and (time.monotonic()-no_improve_time > 30 or iterations % 200 ==0) and time.monotonic()-last_lp_time > 60:
           cand, P_cand = stochastic_lp_step(a,c,P,rng)
           if cand is not None and P_cand < P:
               a=cand; P=P_cand; update best; last_lp_time=now; failures=0
           else:
               last_lp_time=now
       # random perturbation if stuck
       if failures > 50 and rng.random()<0.2:
           cand = random_perturb(a,rng,sigma=0.05)
           P_cand=...
           if P_cand < P or (P_cand-P)<1e-3*P and rng.random()<0.1:
               a=cand
       iterations +=1
    # final exact evaluation maybe outside
    return best_a, best_P
```

Let's define `safe_evaluate(seq)`:
```
def safe_evaluate(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(val): return float('inf')
    return val
```

`clamp_sequence(obj)`:
```
def clamp_sequence(obj):
    out=[]
    try: iterator = iter(obj)
    except: return []
    for x in iterator:
       try: y=float(x)
       except: continue
       if not np.isfinite(y): continue
       if y<0: y=0.0
       elif y>1000.0: y=1000.0
       out.append(y)
    return out
```
No bool? We can also skip bool: `isinstance(x,bool)`.

`normalize_sequence(arr)`:
```
def normalize_sequence(arr):
    arr=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim!=1: arr=arr.ravel()
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-12:
       if arr.size>0: return np.ones(arr.size,dtype=np.float64)/arr.size
       return np.ones(1)
    arr=arr/s
    arr[arr<0]=0
    return arr
```

`central_binomial(n)`:
```
def central_binomial(n):
    a=np.empty(n,float)
    a[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    return a
```
For large n, recurrence 100k O(n), okay.

a=central_binomial(n)
x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
if alpha: a=a*np.exp(alpha*x)
# maybe add small random positive perturbation if seed not None? no
return normalize_sequence(a)
```

`next_power_of_two(m)`:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p
```

`convolution_square_fft(a, L=None)`:
```
def convolution_square_fft(a, L=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size; need=2*n-1
    if L is None: L=next_power_of_two(need)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:need]
    c=np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    # remove small negative
    minc=c.min()
    if minc < 0:
        c=c.copy(); c[c<0]=0.0
    return c
```

`active_weights(c, P)`:
```
def active_weights(c, P):
    if not np.isfinite(P) or P <=0:
       w=np.ones(c.size)/c.size; return w
    tol = 0.05 * P
    active = c >= (P - tol)
    if not active.any(): active = c >= (P - max(tol, P*1e-6))
    # If too many? maybe use all. To focus, use exponential within active
    temp = 0.02 * P
    exp_arg = np.full(c.size, -np.inf)
    exp_arg[active] = ((c[active] - P) / temp) + 1.0  # max=1
    w = np.exp(exp_arg)
    s=w.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: w=np.where(active,1,0); s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w=np.ones(c.size)/c.size
    else: w/=s
    return w
```
This exponential may underflow for c far below; fine. If active plateau, all exp(1) equal. If active many, sum large; normalized.

`gradient_from_weights(a, w, L)`:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a,w,L):
    n=a.size; need=2*n-1
    yrev = w[::-1]
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,L); Y=np.fft.rfft(yrev,L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(F*Y,L)
    idx = need-1 - np.arange(n)
    z=conv[idx].astype(np.float64)
    z[z<0]=0
    return z
```

`exp_normalize(theta)`:
```
def exp_normalize(theta):
    theta=np.asarray(theta,float)
    finite=np.isfinite(theta)
    if not finite.all():
       theta=np.where(finite, theta, -745.0)
    m=theta.max()
    if not finite(m) or m < -700:
       return np.ones(theta.size)/theta.size
    v=np.exp(theta-m)
    s=v.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite(s):
       return np.ones(theta.size)/theta.size
    return v/s
```

`positive_normalize_arr` as above.

def approx_score(a, L=None):
    c=convolution_square_fft(a,L)
    P=c.max()
    return float(2*a.size*P), float(P), c
```
But for optimization, we want not allocate score repeatedly with conv? We'll compute c.

`try_soft_update(a, z, eta, alpha)`:
```
def make_soft_candidate(a, z, eta, alpha):
    zmax = z.max()
    if zmax <=0: return None
    zn = z / zmax
    theta = -eta * zn
    g = exp_normalize(theta)
    cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*g
    return normalize_sequence(cand)
```
Could `normalize_sequence` clip to 1000, but values small. Good.

return exp_normalize(theta)

`random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)`:
```
n=a.size; cand=a.copy()
if rng.random()<0.5:
   width=max(2, int(n*rng.uniform(0.01,0.2)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))
   factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma)))
   cand[start:start+width]*=factor
else:
   cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,max(1,n)))
return normalize_sequence(cand)
```

`lp_step(a, c, P, rng, K=None)`:
Requires scipy. We'll import inside.
```
def lp_relax_step(a, c, P, rng, K=None):
    n=a.size; need=2*n-1
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import linprog
       from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    except Exception:
       return None, None
    if K is None:
       K = max(32, min(128, n//32))
    # selected active spread
    tol=0.15*P
    active=np.where(c >= P - tol)[0]
    if active.size ==0: active=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
    if active.size > K:
       # evenly spaced positions
       pos = np.linspace(0, active.size-1, K).astype(int)
       # add small jitter
       jitter = max(1, active.size // (2*K))
       pos = pos + rng.integers(0, jitter+1, K)
       pos = np.clip(pos,0,active.size-1)
       selected = active[pos]
    else:
       selected = active
    selected = selected.astype(int)
    # ensure spread: selected unique, maybe add min and max active
    selected = np.unique(selected)
    K = selected.size
    if K ==0: return None,None
    # Build matrix rows length K
    row_parts=[]; col_parts=[]; data_parts=[]
    for r,k in enumerate(selected):
       i_min = max(0, k - n + 1)
       i_max = min(n - 1, k)
       length = i_max - i_min + 1
       if length <=0: continue
       cols = np.arange(i_min, i_max+1, dtype=np.int32)
       js = k - cols
       data = a[js]
       rows = np.full(length, r, dtype=np.int32)
       row_parts.append(rows); col_parts.append(cols); data_parts.append(data)
    if not row_parts: return None,None
    rows=np.concatenate(row_parts); cols=np.concatenate(col_parts); data=np.concatenate(data_parts)
    A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(K,n))
    b_ub = np.full(K, P, dtype=np.float64)
    obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    # scale obj? no
    try:
       res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0, None), method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       try:
          bounds=[(0, None)]*n
          res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       except Exception:
          return None,None
    if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False): return None,None
    g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g=np.clip(g,0.0,1000.0*max(1.0, 1.0/ (P+1e-12))) # upper? maybe none. use clip 0 only
    gsum=g.sum()
    if gsum <=0 or not finite: return None,None
    g = g/gsum
    L=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    best_cand=None; best_P=None
    for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.05):
       cand=(1-t)*a + t*g
       cand=normalize_sequence(cand)
       Pc=convolution_square_fft(cand,L).max()
       if Pc < P - 1e-14 and (best_P is None or Pc < best_P):
          best_cand=cand; best_P=Pc
    return best_cand, best_P
```
Upper bound g clip: If unbounded, res.x may huge. Normalize, but if one coordinate huge, g nearly delta, candidate may be bad; line search rejects. We can cap g at e.g. `10*np.max(a)/min_positive?` not needed. `np.clip(g,0,None)`.

Potential issue: Linprog with sparse A and `bounds=(0,None)` may interpret None incorrectly? We'll fallback.

`optimize_sequence` function as top-level. It will take `initial_seq` (list or array), `time_budget`, `seed`, `label`.
Need manage exact evaluations? We can pass a callback? To avoid closures, implement exact update inside propose, not inside optimize. Optimize returns best array and approximate P. Then caller exact evaluates.

But optimize may not know if exact available. Fine.

Inside optimize, no exact eval. It uses FFT. At end, returns best_a. It should also return approximate score.

Pseudo code for optimize with LP:
```
def optimize_sequence(initial, time_budget, seed=0, label=''):
    a=normalize_sequence(initial)
    n=a.size
    if n<2 or time_budget <=0: return a, 2.0*n*convolution_square_fft(a).max()
    deadline=time.monotonic()+time_budget
    L=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    c=convolution_square_fft(a,L); P=c.max()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_P=P
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    eta=3.0; alpha=0.25
    failures=0
    no_improve_time=time.monotonic()
    last_lp_time=no_improve_time
    iterations=0
    max_no_improve_seconds=45.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        # ensure c,P current
        c=convolution_square_fft(a,L); P=c.max()
        if P < best_P - 1e-15:
            best_P=P; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve_time=time.monotonic()
        accepted=False
        z=gradient_from_weights(a, active_weights(c,P), L)
        zmax=z.max()
        if zmax > 0 and np.isfinite(zmax):
            g=exp_normalize(-eta*(z/zmax))
            cand=(1-alpha)*a + alpha*g
            cand=normalize_sequence(cand)
            Pc=convolution_square_fft(cand,L).max()
            if Pc < P - 1e-14:
                a=cand; P=Pc; accepted=True
                failures=0
                eta=min(20.0, eta*1.04)
                alpha=min(0.8, alpha*1.02)
            else:
                # try multiplicative alternative
                step=max(0.1, eta*0.1)
                cand2=exp_normalize(np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)) - step*(z/zmax))
                cand2=normalize_sequence(cand2)
                Pc2=convolution_square_fft(cand2,L).max()
                if Pc2 < P - 1e-14:
                    a=cand2; P=Pc2; accepted=True; failures=0
                else:
                    failures +=1
                    eta=max(0.5, eta*0.9)
                    alpha=max(0.05, alpha*0.95)
        else:
            break
        if not accepted:
            # random move if stuck
            if failures > 30 and time.monotonic()-no_improve_time > 20:
                sig = 0.05 * min(1.0, failures/200)
                cand=random_perturb(a,rng,sig)
                Pc=convolution_square_fft(cand,L).max()
                if Pc < P - 1e-14:
                    a=cand; P=Pc; failures=0
                else:
                    # accept worse occasionally if very small
                    if Pc - P < 1e-3*P and rng.random()<0.1:
                        a=cand
                    elif Pc < best_P - 1e-12:
                        pass
        if P < best_P - 1e-15:
            best_P=P; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve_time=time.monotonic()
        # LP periodic
        if P >= best_P - 1e-14 and (failures > 50 or time.monotonic()-no_improve_time > max_no_improve_seconds):
            now=time.monotonic()
            if now - last_lp_time > max(10.0, min(120.0, n/50.0)):
                cand, Pc = lp_relax_step(a,c,P,rng)
                last_lp_time=now
                if cand is not None and Pc < best_P - 1e-14:
                    a=cand; P=Pc; best_a=a.copy(); best_P=Pc; failures=0; no_improve_time=now
                elif cand is not None and Pc < P - 1e-14:
                    a=cand; P=Pc; failures=0
                # if no improvement, continue
        iterations += 1
        # if no improvement for long and LP failed/random failed, maybe break to save time
        if iterations > 10000 and time.monotonic()-no_improve_time > 180:
            break
    return best_a, float(2*n*best_P)
```
Need avoid `c` stale after LP? We compute at loop start.

The LP step uses c current. It may be expensive; condition maybe triggers early. For n=6962, `n/50=139s`, so first LP maybe after 45s no improve or failures. This might be okay. But we might want LP earlier to improve. Set `min_lp_gap = 30s`.

Maybe run an initial batch of LP steps before gradient: At start of optimize, do e.g. 5 stochastic LP steps (if n≤10000 and time_budget>120) to get a better direction. This uses current c. It might be beneficial. But if LP slow, budget. We can do 2 initial LP calls.

`lp_relax_step` is random; multiple calls with different selected active sets. We can loop:
```
for _ in range(num_lp):
   cand,Pc = lp_relax_step(...)
   if improve: update; c=conv...
```
Add in optimize start and periodically. `num_lp` = 3 for n<=4096, 1 for larger.

But if LP takes long, code may timeout. We can check time before each call and budget. But no time limit inside linprog. Use K small (32) for first.

Maybe use `K = max(16, min(64, n//50))` for speed. For n=6962 K=64? n//50=139, min64 =>64. Good. For n=4096 K=64. For n=1024 K=20? max16 min64 =>20. Good.

Let's adjust LP matrix K: `K = max(16, min(96, n//64))`: n=6962 ->96? 6962//64=108, min96. nnz 0.67M, okay. Use 96.

Now, exact evaluation schedule in propose:
- We'll maintain `best_exact_seq`, `best_exact_score`.
- Function `try_update_best(seq, approx_score)` maybe top-level? It can call safe_evaluate if time and exact_calls limit. But no closures. We can implement inside propose with local variables? That's not a closure, just code. It's okay. But helper functions top-level no closures. Local variables fine.

`propose_candidate` can call a top-level `maybe_exact_update(seq, best_score, exact_calls, deadline, max_exact_calls)`? It would need modify best, exact_calls; Python can't modify ints by ref without return. We can write a helper returning `(best_seq,best_score,exact_calls)`. But no closures, top-level okay.

```
def try_exact_update(seq, current_best_score, deadline, max_exact_calls, exact_calls):
    if exact_calls >= max_exact_calls: return seq, current_best_score, exact_calls

def propose_candidate(...):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=...
    best_exact_seq=None; best_exact_score=inf
    best_approx_seq=None; best_approx_score=inf

    h = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    height_seq=[]; height_score=inf
    if h is not None:
       seq=clamp_sequence(h)
       L=len(seq)
       if 2 <= L <= 20000:
          height_seq=seq
          now=time.monotonic()
          if now < deadline-60:
             height_score=safe_evaluate(seq)
             if finite and < best_exact_score: update
          if L not best_approx... 
    # maybe consider height in approx:
    if height_seq:
       a=normalize_sequence(height_seq); P=conv_sq...; score=...; update approx
    # Similarly central tilts will update approx/exact final.

    configs = []
    if height_seq:
       t = 0.35*budget if height_score <1.6 else 0.15*budget
       configs.append((height_seq, len(height_seq), t, 'height'))
    # small central
    n=1024; configs.append((None,n,min(60,budget*0.05),'central_small'))
    primary = len(height_seq) if height_seq else 6962
    if not (1000 <= primary <= 12000): primary = 6962
    configs.append((None, primary, 0.4*budget,'central_primary'))
    configs.append((None, 8192, 0.3*budget,'central_large'))
    # if primary != 4096 maybe alt 4096
    configs.append((None, 4096, 0.15*budget,'central_alt'))

    for idx, (init_seq,n,t_slice) in enumerate(configs):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break
       slice_time = min(t_slice, deadline - time.monotonic() - 30)
       if slice_time < 20: continue
       if init_seq is None:
          init_seq = best_tilted_initial(n, time_limit=min(10, slice_time*0.1))
       # maybe exact update init if fast
       if init_seq is not None:
          # exact eval if n<=20000 and exact_calls<...
          score=safe_evaluate(init_seq)
          update
       a, approx_score = optimize_sequence(init_seq, slice_time, seed=100+idx)
       if a is not None:
          seq = [float(x) for x in a]
          # clamp
          if approx_score < best_approx_score: best_approx... but use exact for final
          # exact eval if time
          now=time.monotonic()
          if now < deadline - 20:
             score=safe_evaluate(seq)
             if finite and < best_exact_score: update
    # final exact for best_approx if not same as best_exact
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       if best_approx_seq is not None and (best_exact_seq is None or best_approx_score < best_exact_score - 1e-12):
          seq=[float(x) for x in best_approx_seq]
          score=safe_evaluate(seq)
          if finite and score < best_exact_score: update
    # final fallback
    if best_exact_seq is not None:
       return [float(x) for x in best_exact_seq]
    if best_approx_seq is not None:
       return [float(x) for x in best_approx_seq]
    return [1.0]
```

`best_tilted_initial`: Need pass expected time? It uses `time.monotonic()` and breaks. If no time, returns central plain.
```
def best_tilted_initial(n, time_limit=5.0, seed=0):
    if n <2: return np.ones(1)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline = time.monotonic()+max(0,time_limit)-1
    cb=central_binomial(n)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    L=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    best_a= normalize_sequence(cb.copy())
    best_score=2*n*convolution_square_fft(best_a,L).max()
    alphas = np.linspace(-2.0,4.0,31)
    for alpha in alphas:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       a=normalize_sequence(cb * np.exp(alpha*x))
       P=convolution_square_fft(a,L).max()
       score=2*n*P
       if score<best_score:
          best_score=score; best_a=a.copy()
    return best_a
```
If alpha=0, same as initial; fine. Could add random perturb for diversity? For initial tilt, we can add a small random skew maybe. But okay.

```python
"""
Search for low-peak nonnegative convolution sequences.

For the displayed evaluator the score is
    2n * max_k (a*a)[k] / (sum_i a_i)^2,
so the problem is equivalent, after fixing sum(a)=1, to minimizing the
largest coefficient of the polynomial square A(z)^2.  A strong constructive
initialization is the truncation of (1-z)^(-1/2), whose asymptotic score is
pi/2.  This solver tilts/optimizes such sequences with FFT-based subgradient
and stochastic relaxed-LP steps.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False


def safe_evaluate(seq):
    """Call evaluate_sequence safely."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def clamp_sequence(obj):
    """Convert an iterable of numbers to a valid nonnegative float list."""
    out = []
    try:
        it = iter(obj)
    except Exception:
        return out
    for x in it:
        if isinstance(x, bool):
            continue
        try:
            y = float(x)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if not np.isfinite(y):
            continue
        if y < 0.0:
            y = 0.0
        elif y > 1000.0:
            y = 1000.0
        out.append(y)
    return out


def normalize_sequence(obj):
    """Clip to [0,1000], remove nonfinite, and normalize sum to 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = arr.copy()
    bad = ~np.isfinite(arr)
    if bad.any():
        arr[bad] = 0.0
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(arr.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(arr.size)
    arr = arr / s
    arr[arr < 0.0] = 0.0
    return arr


def next_power_of_two(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    p = 1
    while p < int(m):
        p <<= 1
    return p


def central_binomial(n):
    """Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), truncated to length n."""
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return a
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    return a


def best_tilted_initial(n, time_limit=8.0, seed=0):
    """Scan exponential tilts of the (1-z)^(-1/2) initialization."""
    if n < 2:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64))

    end = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_limit))
    cb = central_binomial(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    L = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)

    best_a = normalize_sequence(cb.copy())
    best_score = float(2 * n * convolution_square_fft(best_a, L).max())

    alphas = np.linspace(-2.0, 4.0, 31, dtype=np.float64)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if time.monotonic() >= end:
            break
        a = normalize_sequence(cb * np.exp(alpha * x))
        score = float(2 * n * convolution_square_fft(a, L).max())
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()

    # Slightly diversify repeated same-length starts.
    if seed and int(seed) > 0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
        if rng.random() < 0.7:
            best_a = normalize_sequence(best_a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.015, size=n)))

    return best_a


def convolution_square_fft(a, L=None):
    """Nonnegative real coefficients of A(z)^2 via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if L is None:
        L = next_power_of_two(need)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:need]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size and c.min() < 0.0:
        c = c.copy()
        c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def active_weights(c, P):
    """Weights concentrated on near-maximum convolution lags."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(P) or P <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)

    tol = 0.05 * P
    active = c >= (P - tol)
    if not active.any():
        active = c >= (P - max(tol, 1e-12 * P))

    temp = 0.02 * P
    exp_arg = -np.inf
    exp_arg = np.full(c.size, -np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    if active.any():
        exp_arg[active] = ((c[active] - P) / temp) + 1.0
    w = np.exp(np.clip(exp_arg, -80.0, 80.0))
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.where(active, 1.0, 0.0).astype(np.float64)
        s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
    else:
        w = w / s
    return w


def gradient_from_weights(a, w, L):
    """
    Given weights w over convolution lags, return z_i = sum_k w_k a_{k-i}.
    This is the subgradient contribution for reducing the weighted active peaks.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    need = 2 * n - 1
    yrev = w[::-1]
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    Y = np.fft.rfft(yrev, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(F * Y, L)
    idx = (need - 1) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    z = np.asarray(conv[idx], dtype=np.float64)
    z[z < 0.0] = 0.0
    z[~np.isfinite(z)] = 0.0
    return z


def exp_normalize(theta):
    """Stable softmax/exponential normalization to sum 1."""
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if theta.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta = theta.copy()
    theta[~np.isfinite(theta)] = -745.0
    m = float(theta.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < -700.0:
        return np.ones(theta.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(theta.size)
    v = np.exp(theta - m)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(theta.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(theta.size)
    v /= s
    return v


def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.03):
    """Small multiplicative local/global perturbations."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a
    if rng.random() < 0.55:
        w = int(rng.integers(max(2, max(2, n // 80)), max(3, n // 6)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - w + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + w)
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
        a[start:stop] *= factor
    else:
        a *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma * 0.7, size=n))
    return normalize_sequence(a)


def lp_relax_step(a, c, P, rng, K=None):
    """
    Stochastic relaxed LP direction.
    Select a spread of active lags, solve max sum(g) subject to (a*g)_k <= P
    for those lags, then line-search a convex mixture with the current a.
    """
    if not _HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None, None

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if n < 2 or not np.isfinite(P) or P <= 0.0:
        return None, None

    if K is None:
        K = max(16, min(96, n // 64))

    tol = 0.15 * P
    active = np.where(c >= (P - tol))[0]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64)

    if active.size > K:
        pos = np.linspace(0, active.size - 1, K).astype(np.int64)
        jitter = max(1, active.size // (2 * K))
        pos = pos + rng.integers(0, jitter + 1, K)
        pos = np.clip(pos, 0, active.size - 1)
        selected = active[pos]
    else:
        selected = active
    selected = np.unique(selected.astype(np.int64))
    K = int(selected.size)
    if K == 0:
        return None, None

    row_parts = []
    col_parts = []
    data_parts = []

    for r, k in enumerate(selected):
        i_min = max(0, int(k) - n + 1)
        i_max = min(n - 1, int(k))
        length = i_max - i_min + 1
        if length <= 0:
            continue
        cols = np.arange(i_min, i_max + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        js = int(k) - cols
        data = a[js]
        rows = np.full(length, r, dtype=np.int32)
        row_parts.append(rows)
        col_parts.append(cols)
        data_parts.append(data)

    if not row_parts:
        return None, None

    rows = np.concatenate(row_parts)
    cols = np.concatenate(col_parts)
    data = np.concatenate(data_parts)

    A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(K, n))
    A = A / P
    b_ub = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)
    obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    res = None
    try:
        res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0.0, 1e8), method="highs")
    except Exception:
        try:
            bounds = [(0.0, 1e8)] * n
            res = linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds)
        except Exception:
            return None, None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None, None

    g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    g[~np.isfinite(g)] = 0.0
    g[g < 0.0] = 0.0
    s = float(g.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None, None
    g /= s

    L = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    best_cand = None
    best_P = None
    for t in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 0.05, 0.35):
        cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * g
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
        Pc = float(convolution_square_fft(cand, L).max())
        if np.isfinite(Pc) and (best_P is None or Pc < best_P):
            best_P = Pc
            best_cand = cand

    if best_cand is not None:
        return best_cand, float(best_P)
    return None, None


def optimize_sequence(initial, time_budget, seed=0):
    """
    Local optimizer for a fixed length.  Returns (best_array, best_approx_score).
    Uses FFT subgradient updates plus occasional stochastic LP directions.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(initial)
    n = a.size
    if n < 2 or time_budget <= 0.0:
        P = float(convolution_square_fft(a).max())
        return a, float(2.0 * n * P)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_budget))
    L = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    c = convolution_square_fft(a, L)
    P = float(c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_P = P

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    eta = 3.5
    alpha = 0.30
    failures = 0
    no_improve_time = time.monotonic()
    last_lp_time = no_improve_time
    iterations = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        c = convolution_square_fft(a, L)
        P = float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(P) or P <= 0.0:
            break

        if P < best_P - 1e-15:
            best_P = P
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve_time = time.monotonic()

        accepted = False
        if P > 0.0:
            w = active_weights(c, P)
            z = gradient_from_weights(a, w, L)
            zmax = float(z.max())

            if rng.random() < 0.18 and zmax > 0.0:
                z = np.maximum(z + (0.003 * zmax * rng.standard_normal(n)), 0.0)
                zmax = float(z.max())

            if np.isfinite(zmax) and zmax > 0.0:
                zn = z / zmax
                g = exp_normalize(-eta * zn)
                cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * g
                cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
                Pc = float(convolution_square_fft(cand, L).max())
                neutral_tol = max(1e-13, 1e-7 * P)

                if Pc < P - 1e-14:
                    a = cand
                    P = Pc
                    accepted = True
                    failures = 0
                    eta = min(22.0, eta * 1.04)
                    alpha = min(0.85, alpha * 1.03)
                elif Pc <= P + neutral_tol and rng.random() < 0.22:
                    a = cand
                    P = Pc
                    accepted = True
                    failures = min(failures, 4)
                    eta = max(0.5, eta * 0.99)
                    alpha = max(0.05, alpha * 0.99)

                if not accepted:
                    step = max(0.05, 0.25 * eta)
                    cand2 = exp_normalize(np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300)) - step * zn)
                    cand2 = normalize_sequence(cand2)
                    Pc2 = float(convolution_square_fft(cand2, L).max())
                    if Pc2 < P - 1e-14:
                        a = cand2
                        P = Pc2
                        accepted = True
                        failures = 0
                    else:
                        failures += 1
                        eta = max(0.5, eta * 0.90)
                        alpha = max(0.05, alpha * 0.95)

        if P < best_P - 1e-15:
            best_P = P
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve_time = time.monotonic()

        # Stochastic LP polishing, especially when the subgradient stalls.
        if _HAVE_SCIPY and n <= 12000 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
            if (failures > 25 or time.monotonic() - no_improve_time > 25.0):
                now = time.monotonic()
                min_gap = max(15.0, min(75.0, 0.012 * n))
                if now - last_lp_time > min_gap:
                    last_lp_time = now
                    cand, Pc = lp_relax_step(a, c, P, rng)
                    if cand is not None and np.isfinite(Pc):
                        neutral_tol = max(1e-13, 1e-7 * P)
                        if Pc < P - 1e-14:
                            a = cand
                            P = Pc
                            failures = 0
                            no_improve_time = time.monotonic()
                        elif Pc <= P + neutral_tol and rng.random() < 0.25:
                            a = cand
                            P = Pc
                            failures = min(failures, 6)

        # Random escape if stuck.
        if not accepted and failures > 45 and time.monotonic() - no_improve_time > 20.0:
            sigma = max(0.008, 0.035 * min(1.0, 800.0 / max(1.0, n)))
            cand = random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)
            Pc = float(convolution_square_fft(cand, L).max())
            neutral_tol = max(1e-13, 1e-6 * P)
            if Pc < P - 1e-14:
                a = cand
                P = Pc
                failures = 0
                no_improve_time = time.monotonic()
            elif Pc <= P + neutral_tol and rng.random() < 0.15:
                a = cand
                P = Pc
                failures = min(failures, 8)

        iterations += 1
        if iterations > 12000 and time.monotonic() - no_improve_time > 240.0:
            break

    return best_a, float(2.0 * n * best_P)


def to_float_list(arr):
    """Convert final candidate to a plain Python list of finite nonnegative floats."""
    out = []
    for x in np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel():
        if not np.isfinite(x):
            continue
        if x < 0.0:
            x = 0.0
        elif x > 1000.0:
            x = 1000.0
        out.append(float(x))
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Entry point.  Searches several lengths/initializations within the time budget
    and returns the best valid sequence found.
    """
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or float(budget_s) <= 0.0:
        return [1.0]

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(budget_s)) - 45.0
    base_seed = int(seed) if np.isfinite(seed) else 17

    best_exact_seq = None
    best_exact_score = float("inf")
    best_approx_seq = None
    best_approx_score = float("inf")
    exact_calls = 0
    max_exact_calls = 9

    # Use the provided construction if it is valid and not absurdly long.
    height_seq = None
    height_score = float("inf")
    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if h is not None:
        hs = clamp_sequence(h)
        if 64 <= len(hs) <= 12000:
            height_seq = hs
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0 and exact_calls < max_exact_calls:
                score = safe_evaluate(hs)
                exact_calls += 1
                if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_exact_score:
                    best_exact_score = score
                    best_exact_seq = list(hs)
                height_score = score
            arr = normalize_sequence(hs)
            P = float(convolution_square_fft(arr).max())
            approx = float(2.0 * arr.size * P)
            if approx < best_approx_score:
                best_approx_score = approx
                best_approx_seq = arr.copy()

    # Build search configs.  The height sequence is prioritized if it already looks good.
    configs = []
    if height_seq is not None:
        if height_score < 1.60:
            configs.append((height_seq, len(height_seq), 0.42, base_seed + 11, "height"))
        else:
            configs.append((height_seq, len(height_seq), 0.18, base_seed + 11, "height"))

    # A longer sequence usually gives more room to reduce the peak/average ratio.
    primary = len(height_seq) if height_seq is not None else 6962
    if not (2000 <= primary <= 9000):
        primary = 6962
    configs.append((None, primary, 0.35, base_seed + 12, "central_primary"))

    configs.append((None, 8192, 0.30, base_seed + 13, "central_large"))
    configs.append((None, 2048, 0.16, base_seed + 14, "central_small2"))
    configs.append((None, 1024, 0.08, base_seed + 15, "central_small1"))

    # Avoid duplicate work for identical lengths with same source type.
    seen = set()
    ordered = []
    for cfg in configs:
        init_seq, n, frac, s, tag = cfg
        key = (int(n), "height" if init_seq is not None else "central")
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        ordered.append(cfg)

    total_budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 45.0)

    print(f"[search] budget={float(budget_s):.1f}s, deadline={deadline - start:.1f}s")
    for idx, (init_seq, n, frac, s, tag) in enumerate(ordered):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 35.0:
            break

        slice_time = min(max(0.0, frac * total_budget), max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 35.0))
        if slice_time < 25.0:
            continue

        if init_seq is not None:
            initial = normalize_sequence(init_seq)
        else:
            if n < 64 or n > 12000:
                continue
            initial = best_tilted_initial(n, time_limit=min(8.0, 0.12 * slice_time), seed=s)

        if initial is None or initial.size < 2:
            continue

        # Exact-check the initial candidate if cheap.
        if n <= 9000 and exact_calls < max_exact_calls and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            seq0 = to_float_list(initial)
            sc0 = safe_evaluate(seq0)
            exact_calls += 1
            if np.isfinite(sc0) and sc0 < best_exact_score:
                best_exact_score = sc0
                best_exact_seq = seq0
                print(f"[search] {tag} n={n} initial exact={sc0:.9f}")

        arr0 = normalize_sequence(initial)
        P0 = float(convolution_square_fft(arr0).max())
        approx0 = float(2.0 * arr0.size * P0)
        if approx0 < best_approx_score:
            best_approx_score = approx0
            best_approx_seq = arr0.copy()

        print(f"[search] {tag} n={n} slice={slice_time:.1f}s approx_initial={approx0:.9f} best_exact={best_exact_score:.9f}")

        best_a, approx_score = optimize_sequence(initial, slice_time, seed=s + idx)
        if best_a is not None:
            if approx_score < best_approx_score:
                best_approx_score = approx_score
                best_approx_seq = best_a.copy()

            # Exact evaluation after optimization, if within budget.
            if exact_calls < max_exact_calls and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
                seq = to_float_list(best_a)
                sc = safe_evaluate(seq)
                exact_calls += 1
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                    best_exact_score = sc
                    best_exact_seq = seq
                    print(f"[search] {tag} n={best_a.size} optimized exact={sc:.9f}")
                else:
                    print(f"[search] {tag} n={best_a.size} optimized approx={approx_score:.9f} exact={sc:.9f}")
            else:
                print(f"[search] {tag} n={best_a.size} optimized approx={approx_score:.9f} (no exact check)")

        # If we are already at essentially the stated target, stop.
        if np.isfinite(best_exact_score) and best_exact_score < 1.5022:
            print(f"[search] early stop: best_exact={best_exact_score:.9f}")
            break

    # Final exact verification of the best approximate sequence.
    if best_approx_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0:
        seq = to_float_list(best_approx_seq)
        sc = safe_evaluate(seq)
        exact_calls += 1
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact_seq = seq
            print(f"[final] exact best={sc:.9f}")
        else:
            print(f"[final] exact check={sc:.9f}, keeping={best_exact_score:.9f}")

    if best_exact_seq is not None:
        return [float(x) for x in best_exact_seq]

    if best_approx_seq is not None:
        return to_float_list(best_approx_seq)

    return [1.0]
```
